So g_v formula correct:
```
def grad_unit(v,h,N,n):
  g=np.zeros(2*n-1)
  vN=N+v
  # target: m=v..vN, add 1 - h[vN - m]
  idx = np.arange(v, vN+1)
  g[idx] += 1.0 - h[vN - idx]
  # source: m=N..N+n-1-v, subtract h[m-N+v]
  if v < n:
     m2 = np.arange(N, N+n-v) # len n-v
     g[m2] -= h[m2 - N + v]
  return g
```

```
  c = correlate(h,1-h)  # raw
  target = n/2

  for pass:

    gp = grad_unit(p,h,c? N,n)
    quad_base = zeros(L); quad_base[N] = -2.0? dependent on i/p.
    for i in order (random or shuffled): if i==p continue
      a = h[i]; pval = h[p]
      # b range: b in [0,1], p_new = pval - (b-a) in [0,1]
      low = max(0.0, a - pval)
      high = min(1.0, a + 1.0 - pval)
```

Evaluation helper for scalar:
```
def eval_c_delta(c, lin, work, d, N, c1, c2):
    work[:] = c
    if d != 0:
       work += d*lin
       d2=d*d
       work[N] += -2*d2
       work[c1] += d2
       work[c2] += d2
    return float(work.max())
```
But `work += d*lin` modifies work in place; if d=0 skip. For grid matrix:
```
def eval_grid_max(c, lin, ds, N, c1, c2):
    vals = c[None,:] + ds[:,None]*lin
    # Copy? c[None,:] creates view? Adding ds[:,None]*lin creates new temp; vals new. Fine for 33*1000.
    d2=ds*ds
    vals[:,N] -= 2*d2
    vals[:,c1] += d2
    vals[:,c2] += d2
    return vals.max(axis=1)
```

```
def slqp_active(h,n,deadline,maxiter,topK=20,tol=1e-7):
  h=project_feasible(h,n)
  c=compute_raw_c(h)
  dx=2/n
  t = c.max()*dx + 1e-8
  active = top shifts with c >= max - max(1e-8,0.002*t)
  # exclude too many? topK
  y = np.concatenate([h,[t]])
  def obj_fun(z): return z[-1]
  def obj_jac(z): jac zeros(n+1); jac[-1]=1
  def cons_fun(z): hh=z[:-1]; raw=compute_raw_c(hh); return raw[active]*dx - z[-1]
  def cons_jac(z): rows... compute deriv g_v? We can compute for active shifts using formula derivative row.
```

```
row=np.zeros(n)
if k>=0:
  # source n=0..n-1-k, target n+k
  row[0:n-k] += -h[k:n]
  row[k:n] += 1-h[0:n-k]
else:
  kk=-k
  # source n=kk..n-1, target n-kk
  row[kk:n] += -h[0:n-kk]
  row[0:n-kk] += 1-h[kk:n]
row*=dx
```

Pivot selection:
```
def choose_pivot(h, rng, top_frac=True):
   center_score = np.minimum(h,1-h)
   maxscore = center_score.max()
   if maxscore > 1e-9:
      candidates = np.where(center_score > max(1e-4, 0.2*maxscore))[0]
      if len(candidates)>1: return rng.choice(candidates)
      return int(np.argmax(center_score))
   else:
      return int(np.argmin(np.minimum(h,1-h))) # binary
```

Let's examine coordinate descent local optimum: It only changes two variables with one pivot; not arbitrary pairs. Is it fully capable? Since sum fixed, any feasible direction is a combination of pair moves; coordinate with pivot sweeps all variables relative to pivot, equivalent to optimizing over all single coordinate moves while preserving sum via pivot. It cannot move pivot itself with a non-pivot unless pivot's value changes as reaction. Over multiple passes, pivot can move. Should be general enough.

Could there be a better direct search: For each pair (i,j), move mass δ (h_i += δ, h_j -=δ) and minimize f(δ). That's symmetric sum-one-year satisfying without pivot dependency? This is exactly a two-variable coordinate. It doesn't require a fixed pivot; for each pair, δ direction. We can perform pair coordinate descent: for selected pairs, compute lin = g_i - g_j, quad as above (same cross with i,j and same -2 at N); δ range based on both bounds. This is cleaner and can move any pair, no pivot. Complexity per pair O(L eval). We can't check all n^2 pairs, but can choose pairs involving fractional/slack or random. A pivot pass is a star of pair moves with pivot; pair coordinate with random pairs could be more flexible. We can implement random pair descent: for each iteration, choose random pair (i,j) with h_i+h_j not both? But to reduce max, maybe choose pairs where one has high and one low? We can do "pair coordinate descent" selecting pairs randomly and optimizing δ. This is similar to pivot but no need pivot interior; for binary, pair swap (i 1, j 0) δ ∈ [-h_i,1-h_j], range includes moving mass both ways. It is exactly feasible. This may be easier and more powerful!

Let's explore two-variable pair move (swap mass) as exact local search. For pair (i,j), let δ = amount added to i, subtract from j: h_i+=δ, h_j-=δ. Sum constant. Objective f(δ) exact with same lin = g_i - g_j, quad same -2 at N, +1 at N±(j-i). δ range [max(0,h_j), min(1,h_i)-? Actually h_i+δ≤1 -> δ≤1-h_i; h_j-δ≥0 -> δ≥δ min = -h_j; lower=-h_j, upper=1-h_i. Includes 0. We can optimize δ ∈ [low, high] exactly with grid. This is simpler than pivot; no pivot index. It can move mass either direction between any pair. It is the natural local search preserving sum. We can perform many pair moves: choose pairs randomly, perhaps biased to boundary/interesting. This is like stochastic coordinate pair descent. It may escape pivot constraints. Cost per move same as coordinate. We can run a loop until time: choose a batch of pairs, optimize δ. If improvement, apply; if not, skip. This is more flexible and works for binary: pair 1/0, δ can move mass (becomes fractional). Great.

Pair move update formula:
- low = -h[j] (δ can be negative to move from i to j? If δ negative, i decreases, j increases), high = 1 - h[i].

- `lin = g_i - g_j` (same as δ positive to i, negative to j). `quad`: -2 at N; +1 at N+(j-i), +1 at N+(i-j). Since δ_i=δ, δ_j=-δ, cross Δ_iΔ_j=-δ^2. same.
- Find best delta over [low, high] with current max. Update c,h.

At c_vec, max shifts. For each active shift m (k), the terms contributing are pairs (n, n+k). We can identify top few contributing pairs for each active shift and do pair coordinate optimization between them.
Algorithm:
- Compute c, active indices top A (e.g., 10).

We can for top L contributing pairs (by contrib) choose pair (source,target) and run pair move. This directly attacks max constraints.
This is a powerful heuristic, different from previous. It focuses on active shifts and worst pairs. It can also discover moves for inactive shifts.

```
def shift_contrib_pairs(h,n,m,num_pairs):
  N=n-1; k=m-N
  if k>=0:
    n0=0; n1=n-k-1
    src = np.arange(n0,n1+1); tgt=src+k
  else:
    kk=-k; src=np.arange(kk,n); tgt=src-kk
  contrib = h[tgt] * (1.0 - h[src])
  # Select top indices where contrib large, maybe also if contrib >0.1 max
  if contrib.size>num_pairs: use np.argpartition
  return list zip(src[t], tgt[t])
```

Wait pair (i,j), δ positive adds to i, subtracts j. To increase src and decrease tgt, with i=src, j=tgt, need δ positive if src can increase, tgt decrease. But if src low, h_src low, i=src can increase up to 1; j=tgt high can decrease. δ positive limited by 1-h_src and h_tgt. Good.

Need implement `pair_move` with in-place update. We need `fill_grad` for i,j. For each pair, recompute grads from current h. h changes each successful move; pair list may become outdated but okay.
```
def do_pair_move(h,c,i,j,N,n,grid_num,refine_levels,refine_num,work,gi,gj,lin):
  a=c.max()
  fill_grad(i,...,gi); fill_grad(j,...,gj); lin=gi-gj
  c1=N+(j-i); c2=N+(i-j)
  low=max(float(-h[i]), float(h[j]-1.0)); high=min(float(1.0-h[i]), float(h[j]))
  best_d,best_f = find_best_delta(c,lin,a,N,c1,c2,low,high,grid_num,refine_levels,refine_num,work)
  if best_d==0 or best_f > a - 1e-14: return False
  # update
  d2=best_d*best_d
  c += best_d*lin
  c[N] -= 2*d2
  c[c1] += d2
  c[c2] += d2
  h[i] += best_d; h[j] -= best_d
  # clip tiny
  if h[i]<0: h[i]=0 ... But bounds should ensure; due rounding.
  return True
```
Need if c1 or c2 out of range? j-i between -(n-1),n-1, so N+k in 0..L-1. Good.

If low/high interval very small, skip. Also if pair both current same? Bounds still.

`find_best_delta` details:
```
def find_best_delta(c,lin,cur_max,N,c1,c2,lo,hi,grid_num,refine_levels,refine_num,work):
  best_d=0.0; best_f=cur_max
  if hi-lo < 1e-13: return...
  # full grid
  ds = np.linspace(lo,hi,grid_num)
  vals = grid_eval(...)
  # include d=0 if lo<0<hi
  f0 = cur_max
  # We can set vals at d=0? Simpler after vals:
  idx=argmin(vals); if vals[idx] < best_f: update
  if lo <= 0 <= hi and cur_max < best_f: update 0.
  A=lo; B=hi
  for lev in range(refine_levels):
    width=B-A
    if width < 1e-12: break
    frac=0.18 # keep ~18% around best? Factor ~1/5.5. Maybe 0.2.
    half=width*frac
    newA=max(lo, best_d-half); newB=min(hi, best_d+half)
    if newB-newA < 1e-12: break
    # ensure if best_d near boundary, interval at least one side: if newA==newB, expand
    ds=np.linspace(newA,newB,refine_num)
    vals=grid_eval; update
    A,B=newA,newB
  # evaluate endpoints and 0
  for candidate in [lo,hi,0.0]:
    if lo<=cand<=hi: val=eval_scalar; update
  if best_f > cur_max + 1e-12: best_d=0.0
  return best_d,best_f
```
But if the true minimum is not within the shrinking interval selected around current best due initial grid error, we might miss. To mitigate, `frac` not too small: 0.35 (keep 35%) for 5 levels gives final ~0.35^5=0.005 of width, high. With refine 11 points step final ~0.0005*width? Full grid 25 step 0.04; after 5*0.35, the interval contains best within 35% of previous if full circle initial best near true. But if full best has error half grid step, 0.35 width may exclude true. Better keep interval width = 2 * grid cell around best. Cell=width/(grid_num-1). Set half = grid_cell * 1.5 maybe. For full width 1, grid_num=25 cell 0.042; half 0.063. Then each refine half = cell *1.5 / refine? Let's implement dynamic:
- After full grid, cell = (B-A)/(grid_num-1); half = 1.5*cell (or 2*cell) around best; new interval [best-half,best+half]. Then refine levels with refine_num, cell2=(newB-newA)/(refine_num-1), half=1.5*cell2. This shrinks by factor cell2/cell ~ (3*cell/refine_num)/cell? For refine_num=11, factor ~3/11=0.27. Good. This is safer: never exclude if error <2 cells.
So for each level, compute interval around best with half = 1.4 * (current_interval_width / (num_points -1)). For first current_width = (hi-lo)/(grid_num-1). For subsequent, width of previous. Implementation:
```
cur_width = hi-lo; num=grid_num
for ...:
  cell=cur_width/(num-1)
  half=1.6*cell
  newA=max(lo,best_d-half); newB=min(hi,best_d+half)
  if newB-newA < max(1e-12, 0.05*half?)...
  if newB-newA <=0: newA=lo? Actually if best at boundary, half one side: newB=best+2half? Let's handle:
     if newA==newB: newA=max(lo,best_d-2*half); newB=min(hi,best_d+2*half)
  ds...
  cur_width=newB-newA; num=refine_num
```
This cancels nonunimodal risk if full best within one cell. Good.

Time loop control:
```
def run_targeted_pairs(h,n,deadline,seed, grid_num=None,...):
  h=h.copy(); c=compute_raw_c(h); best_c5=...
  rng=...
  while time<deadline:
    rawmax = c.max(); thresh=rawmax - max(1e-10, 0.02*rawmax)
    active = np.argsort(-c)[:min(12, len(c))] maybe top 10 with threshold? Use top12.
    pairs=[]
    for m in active:
       src,tgt,contrib = shift_arrays
       if contrib.size: top = argpartition(contrib, -8)
       add
    # if pairs empty, random
    # limit pairs to maybe 60, sample if too many
    improved=False
    # sort pairs by potential? maybe by abs(h_i-h_j) descending (large swap potential)
    for pair in pairs[:max_pairs]:
       if time>deadline: break
       old_max = c.max()
       if do_pair_move(...): improved=True
       # optional if improvements small break?
    # recompute c every outer loop (or every 20 moves?)
    c=compute_raw_c(h); c5=...
    if c5 < best_c5 - 1e-10: best_c5=c5; improved=True
    if not improved:
       # random pairs attempt: choose 100 random pairs, if improve
       if not random_improve(h,c,...):
```

Define top-level SLSQP functions:
```
def slqp_obj(z,n,active,dx): return float(z[-1])
def slqp_obj_grad(z,n,active,dx):
  g=np.zeros(n+1); g[-1]=1.0; return g
def slqp_ineq(z,n,active,dx):
  raw=compute_raw_c(z[:-1])
  return z[-1] - raw[active]*dx
def slqp_ineq_jac(z,n,active,dx):
  h=z[:-1]; n_=len(h); N=n_-1; L=2*n_-1
  J=np.zeros((len(active), n_+1))
  for row,m in enumerate(active):
     k=m-N
     r=np.zeros(n_)
     if k>=0:
       nv=n_-k
       r[0:nv] += -h[k:n_]
       r[k:n_] += 1.0 - h[0:nv]
     else:
       kk=-k; nv=n_-kk
       r[kk:n_] += -h[0:nv]
       r[0:nv] += 1.0 - h[kk:n_]
     J[row,0:n_] = dx*r
  return J
def slqp_eq(z,n): return z[:-1].sum() - n/2.0
def slqp_eq_jac(z,n):
  g=np.zeros(n+1); g[0:n]=1.0; return g
```
Important: For k>=0, c_m = ∑_{n=0}^{n-k-1} h_{n+k}(1-h_n). Derivative:
 source n (1-h): target index n+k => 1-h[n], for n=0..n-k-1 -> r[k:n] += 1-h[0:nv]
 target? c term h_target*(1-h_source); derivative wrt source (1-h_source?) Wait derivative of h_{n+k}(1-h_n) wrt h_n: -h_{n+k}; wrt h_{n+k}: 1-h_n. So code correct. For k<0, kk, n=kk..n-1, target n-kk: r[n] (source) += -h[target]= -h[0:nv]; r[target] +=1-h[source]=1-h[kk:n]. Code correct.
This matches gradient rows for c_m. Good.
Bounds: `[(0,1)]*n + [(0,2)]` maybe t upper 1? C5 max ≤1? Could be <=? h in [0,1], integral 1, c_k≤? Each c_k = ∫ h(...) ≤∫_{overlap}h≤1, so t≤1. Use upper 1.0.
Equality constraint. SLSQP may not keep equality exact; final project.
`minimize(slqp_obj, y0, jac=slqp_obj_grad, args=(n,active,dx), bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, method='SLSQP', options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'ftol': 1e-14})`. In newer SciPy, `ftol` okay, `disp` removed? Use `options={'maxiter':maxiter,'tol':1e-12}` maybe. Provide both? `ftol` recognized? To be safe, use `options={'maxiter': maxiter}` only? SLSQP default tol 1e-8; may not enough. Use `tol` if available? SciPy minimize SLSQP options include `maxiter`, `ftol`, `disp`, `iprint`, `acc`, `ftol`. We can set `ftol=1e-14`.

`grid_eval` function:
```
def grid_eval(c, lin, ds, N, c1, c2):
    ds = np.asarray(ds, dtype=float)
    base = c[None, :] + ds[:, None] * lin
    d2 = ds * ds
    base[:, N] -= 2.0 * d2
    base[:, c1] += d2
    base[:, c2] += d2
    return np.max(base, axis=1)
```
If c1==c2? no. If c1 or c2 maybe out? no. If base length 0? not.

Could there be multiple contributions at c1/c2 if (j-i) shift same as another? only one ordered pair. Good.

`eval_delta_scalar` for endpoints:
```
def eval_delta_scalar(c,lin,d,N,c1,c2,work):
    work[:] = c
    if d != 0:
        work += d*lin
        d2=d*d
        work[N] -= 2*d2
        work[c1] += d2
        work[c2] += d2
    return float(np.max(work))
```
Use in find best for candidates; but requires work preallocated. We can pass work.

```
def find_best_delta(c, lin, cur_max, N, c1, c2, lo, hi, grid_num, refine_levels, refine_num, work):
    best_d=0.0; best_f=cur_max
    # candidate 0 if within
    # full grid

    fvals = grid_eval(...)
    if fvals.size:
       idx=int(np.argmin(fvals)); if fvals[idx] < best_f - tol: update
    # evaluate 0 scalar if lo<=0<=hi and maybe not in grid
    if lo <= 0 <= hi:
       val=eval_scalar(0); compare
    # evaluate lo,hi
    # refine
    A=lo; B=hi; num=grid_num
    for _ in range(refine_levels):
       width=B-A
       if width < 1e-12: break
       cell = width / max(1, num-1)
       half = 1.5 * cell
       if half <= 0: break
       newA = max(lo, best_d - half)
       newB = min(hi, best_d + half)
       if newB - newA < max(1e-13, 0.1*half):
          # expand around best within bounds
          half2 = max(width*0.2, 1e-8)
          newA=max(lo,best_d-half2); newB=min(hi,best_d+half2)
       if newB - newA <= 1e-13: break
       ds = np.linspace(newA,newB,refine_num)
       fvals = grid_eval...
       update
       A,B = newA,newB; num=refine_num
    # final endpoints/0 maybe
    if best_f > cur_max + 1e-12: best_d=0
    return best_d,best_f
```

Detailed functions:

```
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time

def compute_raw_c(h):
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')

def compute_c5_raw(c, n):
    return float(np.max(c) * (2.0 / n))

def compute_c5(h,n):
    return compute_c5_raw(compute_raw_c(h), n)

def project_feasible(h, n):
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    u = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    # binary search lambda
    u = np.clip(u, -10.0, 10.0)  # not necessary
    lo = -float(np.max(u)) - 1.0
    hi = 1.0 - float(np.min(u)) + 1.0
    # Actually for sum 0, lambda <= -max(u); for sum n, lambda >=1-min(u). Could use lo=-max(u)-1, hi=1-min(u)+1.
    for _ in range(80):
       mid=(lo+hi)/2
       s=np.clip(u+mid,0.0,1.0).sum()
       if s < target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    lam=(lo+hi)/2
    x=np.clip(u+lam,0.0,1.0)
    diff=target-x.sum()
    for _ in range(20):
       if abs(diff) < 1e-14: break
       mask=(x>1e-12)&(x<1.0-1e-12)
       if mask.any():
          x[mask] += diff / np.count_nonzero(mask)
          x=np.clip(x,0,1)
       else:
          if diff > 0:
             idx=int(np.argmin(x)); add=min(1.0-x[idx], diff); x[idx]+=add; diff-=add
          else:
             idx=int(np.argmax(x)); sub=min(x[idx], -diff); x[idx]-=sub; diff+=sub
       diff=target-x.sum()
    return x
```
Binary search monotone: If s < target, need larger λ, so lo=mid; else hi=mid. Starting lo maybe sum 0 at -max(u)-1; hi sum n at 1-min(u)+1. But if u large? clip. Good. 80 iterations enough. Final diff maybe due no interior; loop corrects. If target= n/2 in [0,n], projection feasible.
Potential issue: If u contains nan? no.

Now, `fill_grad`:
```
def fill_grad(v, h, N, n, out):
    out.fill(0.0)
    # target slice v : N+v+1 length n
    out[v:N+v+1] += 1.0 - h[N::-1]
    # source slice N-v : N+n-v length n
    out[N-v:N+n-v] -= h
```
Check slice end `N+n-v` exclusive? Source range m=N-v to N+n-1-v inclusive length n. Python end = N+n-v. Yes.
Target end = N+v+1. For v=0,N=1,n=2: end=2; good. For v=n-1,N=n-1: end=N+n-1+1=2n-2? L=2n-1, max index 2n-2, exclusive end 2n-2? Wait length should n: start v=n-1=N, end=N+v+1 = (n-1)+(n-1)+1 =2n-1? `do_pair_move`:
```
def do_pair_move(h, c, i, j, n, grid_num, refine_levels, refine_num, work, gi, gj, lin):
    N=n-1; L=2*n-1
    hi_i = float(h[i]); hj_j=float(h[j])
    lo = max(-hi_i, hj_j - 1.0)
    hi = min(1.0 - hi_i, hj_j)
    if hi - lo < 1e-14: return False
    # margin to avoid bounds
    eps=1e-13
    lo = max(lo, -hi_i + eps)??? If we add margin, might miss exact boundary 0/1. But bounds safe. We can not margin; after update clip.
    cur_max = float(np.max(c))
    fill_grad(i,h,N,n,gi); fill_grad(j,h,N,n,gj); np.subtract(gi,gj,out=lin)
    c1 = N + (j - i); c2 = N + (i - j)
    best_d,best_f = find_best_delta(c,lin,cur_max,N,c1,c2,lo,hi,grid_num,refine_levels,refine_num,work)
    if best_d == 0.0 or best_f >= cur_max - 1e-11:
        return False
    d2=best_d*best_d
    c += best_d * lin
    c[N] -= 2.0*d2
    c[c1] += d2
    c[c2] += d2
    hi_new = hi_i + best_d
    hj_new = hj_j - best_d
    # clamp preserving pair sum
    s = hi_i + hj_j
    hi_new = min(1.0, max(0.0, hi_new))
    hj_new = s - hi_new
    if hj_new < 0.0:
       hj_new=0.0; hi_new = min(1.0, s)
    elif hj_new > 1.0:
       hj_new=1.0; hi_new = max(0.0, s-1.0)
    h[i]=hi_new; h[j]=hj_new
    return True
```
If pair sum s maybe slightly not? h_i+h_j. Using s preserves. If s outside [0,2] due infeasible? h bounds => s in [0,2]. This correction may alter h slightly and pair sum exact for pair but not full sum? It preserves pair sum, so full sum preserved.
If `best_f` computed using unclamped hi/hj; if clipping changes h, objective might slightly change. But clipping only if d at boundary by rounding. It could increase max by tiny. We recompute c later. If one pair moves attempt worsens, no big. We used `cur_max - 1e-11`; if improvement tiny, skip. Good.
But after clipping, c update based on unclamped d might not exactly match h. Rounding negligible. Could if d exactly to boundary and `hi_new` clips to 1 while d slightly over; could create error. Margin maybe better: set `lo = max(lo, -hi_i + 1e-12); hi = min(hi, 1.0-hi_i -1e-12); hi = min(hi, hj_j -1e-12); lo=max(lo, hj-1 -? )` Use `margin=1e-12` only if hi-lo >3*margin. `choose_pivot`:
```
def choose_pivot(h, rng):
    score = np.minimum(h, 1.0-h)
    maxs = float(score.max())
    if maxs > 1e-8:
       thr = max(1e-6, 0.3*maxs)
       cand = np.where(score >= thr)[0]
       if len(cand) > 0:
           return int(rng.choice(cand))
    return int(np.argmax(score))
```
If all binary, argmax score 0 random among? Could choose deterministic 0. Better choose random from max score if tie:
```
cand=np.where(score >= maxs - 1e-12)[0]; return int(rng.choice(cand))
```

`run_pivot_sweeps`:
```
def run_pivot_sweeps(h,n,deadline,seed,grid_num,refine_levels,refine_num):
   h0=h.copy(); c0=compute_raw_c(h0); best_c=compute_c5_raw(c0,n); best_h=h0.copy()
   c=c0; work,...
   rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
   pass_no=0
   while time.time() < deadline:
      p=choose_pivot(h,rng)
      order = rng.permutation(n)
      if p in order? We can iterate and skip p.
      improved=False
      attempts=0
      for i in order:
         if time.time()>deadline: break
         if i==p: continue
         if abs(h[i]-h[p]) < 1e-12: continue
         if do_pair_move(h,c,i,p,n,...): improved=True; attempts+=1
         if attempts>0 and (attempts % 50==0):
            c=compute_raw_c(h); # keep exact
            cur=compute_c5_raw(c,n)
            if cur < best_c - 1e-12: best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
      c=compute_raw_c(h); cur=...
      if cur<best_c: update
      if not improved: break
      pass_no+=1
   # final ensure best_h? If current cur>=best_c, return best_h,best_c.
   return best_h, best_c
```

Need `get_shift_pairs` returns top pairs canonical? Let's implement:
```
def get_shift_pairs(h,n,m,topk=8):
    N=n-1; k=m-N
    if k>=0:
       nv=n-k
       if nv<=0: return empty
       src=np.arange(nv); tgt=src+k
    else:
       kk=-k; nv=n-kk
       src=np.arange(kk,n); tgt=src-kk
    contrib = h[tgt]*(1.0-h[src])
    if contrib.size <= topk:
       order=np.argsort(-contrib)
    else:
       part=np.argpartition(contrib, -topk)[-topk:]
       order=part[np.argsort(-contrib[part])]
    return src[order], tgt[order]
```
If contrib all zero, still returns; do_pair_move may not improve because h diff maybe 0? If contrib zero due h_tgt=0 or h_src=1; but pair with h diff not zero? E.g., h_src=1,h_tgt=0 => contrib=0 but pair could be useful for other shifts; random covers.
Add pairs only if `contrib[val] > 1e-6` maybe? But small contributions could still matter? For active max, top may be small if c small near end; but large improvements likely top. Use threshold `1e-8`.

Now, smooth perturb:
```
def smooth_perturb(h,n,eps,rng):
    h = h.copy()
    if n < 2: return h
    i = np.arange(n)
    d = np.zeros(n)
    max_f = min(5, n//3)
    for f in range(1, max_f+1):
       amp = eps / (f * 0.5?)
       amp = eps / float(f)
       ph1 = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi); ph2=...
       w = 2*np.pi*f*i/float(n)
       d += amp*(np.sin(w+ph1)+np.cos(w+ph2))
    d -= d.mean()
    # scale to max abs <= maybe eps*3
    mx=np.max(np.abs(d)); if mx>0: d *= min(1.0, eps*2/mx)? Actually amp already; no need.
    new=project_feasible(h+d, n)
    return new
```
Use multiple freqs random. Eps in h units. If h near bounds, projection handles.

Upsample:

```
def upsample_h(h,n_new):
    n_old=len(h)
    x_old=np.linspace(0,2,n_old,endpoint=False)
    x_new=np.linspace(0,2,n_new,endpoint=False)
    h_new=np.interp(x_new,x_old,h)
    return project_feasible(h_new,n_new)
```

```
def find_active_indices(c, top_k=12, rel_tol=0.01):
   rawmax=float(c.max())
   threshold = rawmax - max(1e-8, rel_tol*rawmax)
   act = np.where(c >= threshold)[0]
   if act.size ==0: act=np.array([np.argmax(c)])
   if act.size > top_k:
      # choose top top_k by value
      sub = c[act]
      idx = np.argsort(-sub)
      return act[idx[:top_k]]
   return act
```

Implementation:
```
def binary_round_start(h,n):
   target=n/2
   h=np.asarray(h)
   idx=np.argsort(-h)
   hb=np.zeros(n)
   full=int(np.floor(target))
   hb[idx[:full]]=1.0
   rem=target-full
   if rem>1e-12: hb[idx[full]] += rem
   hb=np.clip(hb,0,1)
   return project_feasible(hb,n)
```
If n odd, full floor, remainder 0.5. Good.
This may be a good candidate. Include before perturb.

```
def make_fallback_start():
    n=512
    h=np.empty(n,dtype=float)
    for i in range(n):
        x=i; c=0
        while x:
            c += x & 1
            x >>= 1
        h[i] = float(c & 1)
    return h
```

```
def smooth_perturb(h,n,eps,rng):
    x=f.n.copy()
    idx=np.arange(n)
    d=np.zeros(n)
    maxf=min(6, n//3)
    for f in range(1,maxf+1):
       amp = eps / float(f)
       p1=rng.uniform(0.0,2.0*np.pi); p2=...
       w = (2.0*np.pi*float(f)*idx)/float(n)
       d += amp * (np.sin(w+p1) + np.cos(w+p2))
    d -= d.mean()
    # scale max to 4*eps maybe
    mx = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
    if mx > 1e-15:
       scale = min(1.0, (3.0*eps)/mx)
       if scale < 1.0: d *= scale
    return project_feasible(x + d, n)
```

```python
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize
import time


def compute_raw_c(h):
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")


def compute_c5_raw(c, n):
    return float(np.max(c) * (2.0 / float(n)))


def compute_c5(h, n):
    return compute_c5_raw(compute_raw_c(h), n)


def project_feasible(h, n):
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    u = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    if u.size == 0:
        return u

    lo = -float(np.max(u)) - 1.0
    hi = 1.0 - float(np.min(u)) + 1.0

    for _ in range(90):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        s = float(np.clip(u + mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum())
        if s < target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    lam = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
    x = np.clip(u + lam, 0.0, 1.0)

    for _ in range(50):
        diff = target - float(x.sum())
        if abs(diff) < 1e-14:
            break

        mask = (x > 1e-12) & (x < 1.0 - 1e-12)
        cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
        if cnt > 0:
            x[mask] = x[mask] + (diff / cnt)
            x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)
        else:
            if diff > 0.0:
                idxs = np.where(x < 1.0 - 1e-12)[0]
                if idxs.size == 0:
                    idxs = np.where(x < 1.0)[0]
                if idxs.size == 0:
                    break
                step = diff / float(idxs.size)
                x[idxs] = x[idxs] + step
            else:
                idxs = np.where(x > 1e-12)[0]
                if idxs.size == 0:
                    idxs = np.where(x > 0.0)[0]
                if idxs.size == 0:
                    break
                step = diff / float(idxs.size)
                x[idxs] = x[idxs] + step
            x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)

    diff = target - float(x.sum())
    if abs(diff) > 1e-15:
        if diff > 0.0:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - x))
            add = min(1.0 - x[idx], diff)
            x[idx] = x[idx] + add
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(x))
            sub = min(x[idx], -diff)
            x[idx] = x[idx] - sub
        x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)

    return x


def make_fallback_start():
    n = 512
    h = np.empty(n, dtype=float)
    for i in range(n):
        x = i
        c = 0
        while x:
            c += x & 1
            x >>= 1
        h[i] = float(c & 1)
    return h


def binary_round_start(h, n):
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    order = np.argsort(-h)
    hb = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    full = int(np.floor(target + 1e-12))
    if full > n:
        full = n
    hb[order[:full]] = 1.0
    rem = target - float(full)
    if rem > 1e-12 and full < n:
        hb[order[full]] = min(1.0, hb[order[full]] + rem)
    return project_feasible(hb, n)


def resample_h(h, n_new):
    n_old = len(h)
    if n_old <= 0:
        return np.zeros(n_new, dtype=float)
    if n_old == n_new:
        return project_feasible(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), n_new)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_old, endpoint=False)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_new, endpoint=False)
    y = np.interp(x_new, x_old, np.asarray(h, dtype=float))
    return project_feasible(y, n_new)


def fill_grad(v, h, N, n, out):
    out.fill(0.0)
    out[v:N + v + 1] += 1.0 - h[N::-1]
    out[N - v:N + n - v] -= h


def grid_eval(c, lin, ds, N, c1, c2):
    ds = np.asarray(ds, dtype=float)
    base = c[None, :] + ds[:, None] * lin
    d2 = ds * ds
    base[:, N] -= 2.0 * d2
    base[:, c1] += d2
    base[:, c2] += d2
    return np.max(base, axis=1)


def eval_delta_scalar(c, lin, d, N, c1, c2, work):
    work[:] = c
    if d != 0.0:
        work += d * lin
        d2 = d * d
        work[N] -= 2.0 * d2
        work[c1] += d2
        work[c2] += d2
    return float(np.max(work))


def find_best_delta(c, lin, cur_max, N, c1, c2, lo, hi,
                    grid_num, refine_levels, refine_num, work):
    if lo > hi:
        lo, hi = hi, lo

    best_d = 0.0
    best_f = float(cur_max)

    if lo <= 0.0 <= hi:
        v = eval_delta_scalar(c, lin, 0.0, N, c1, c2, work)
        if v < best_f:
            best_f = v
            best_d = 0.0

    for cand in (lo, hi):
        if lo <= cand <= hi:
            v = eval_delta_scalar(c, lin, float(cand), N, c1, c2, work)
            if v < best_f:
                best_f = v
                best_d = float(cand)

    if hi - lo < 1e-12:
        return best_d, best_f

    ds = np.linspace(lo, hi, grid_num)
    vals = grid_eval(c, lin, ds, N, c1, c2)
    if vals.size:
        idx = int(np.argmin(vals))
        if vals[idx] < best_f:
            best_f = float(vals[idx])
            best_d = float(ds[idx])

    A = lo
    B = hi
    num = grid_num

    for _ in range(refine_levels):
        width = B - A
        if width < 1e-12:
            break
        cell = width / max(1, num - 1)
        half = 1.5 * cell
        if half <= 0.0:
            break

        newA = max(lo, best_d - half)
        newB = min(hi, best_d + half)
        if newB - newA < max(1e-13, 0.25 * half):
            half2 = max(0.25 * width, 1e-8)
            newA = max(lo, best_d - half2)
            newB = min(hi, best_d + half2)

        if newB - newA <= 1e-13:
            break
        if newA > best_d:
            newA = best_d
        if newB < best_d:
            newB = best_d

        ds = np.linspace(newA, newB, refine_num)
        vals = grid_eval(c, lin, ds, N, c1, c2)
        if vals.size:
            idx = int(np.argmin(vals))
            if vals[idx] < best_f:
                best_f = float(vals[idx])
                best_d = float(ds[idx])

        A = newA
        B = newB
        num = refine_num

    if best_f > float(cur_max) + 1e-12:
        best_d = 0.0
        best_f = float(cur_max)

    return best_d, best_f


def do_pair_move(h, c, i, j, n,
                 grid_num, refine_levels, refine_num,
                 work, gi, gj, lin):
    N = n - 1
    hi_i = float(h[i])
    hj_j = float(h[j])

    lo = max(-hi_i, hj_j - 1.0)
    hi = min(1.0 - hi_i, hj_j)
    if hi - lo < 1e-14:
        return False

    cur_max = float(np.max(c))

    fill_grad(i, h, N, n, gi)
    fill_grad(j, h, N, n, gj)
    np.subtract(gi, gj, out=lin)

    c1 = N + (j - i)
    c2 = N + (i - j)

    best_d, best_f = find_best_delta(
        c, lin, cur_max, N, c1, c2, lo, hi,
        grid_num, refine_levels, refine_num, work
    )

    if best_d == 0.0 or best_f >= cur_max - 1e-12:
        return False

    d2 = best_d * best_d
    c += best_d * lin
    c[N] -= 2.0 * d2
    c[c1] += d2
    c[c2] += d2

    s = hi_i + hj_j
    hi_new = h[i] + best_d
    hj_new = h[j] - best_d

    if hi_new < 0.0:
        hi_new = 0.0
    elif hi_new > 1.0:
        hi_new = 1.0
    hj_new = s - hi_new

    if hj_new < 0.0:
        hj_new = 0.0
        hi_new = min(1.0, s)
    elif hj_new > 1.0:
        hj_new = 1.0
        hi_new = max(0.0, s - 1.0)

    h[i] = float(hi_new)
    h[j] = float(hj_new)
    return True


def get_shift_pairs(h, n, m, topk):
    N = n - 1
    k = int(m) - N
    if k >= 0:
        nv = n - k
        if nv <= 0:
            return np.empty(0, dtype=int), np.empty(0, dtype=int)
        src = np.arange(nv, dtype=int)
        tgt = src + k
    else:
        kk = -k
        nv = n - kk
        if nv <= 0:
            return np.empty(0, dtype=int), np.empty(0, dtype=int)
        src = np.arange(kk, n, dtype=int)
        tgt = src - kk

    contrib = h[tgt] * (1.0 - h[src])
    sz = contrib.size
    if sz <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=int), np.empty(0, dtype=int)

    topk = min(topk, sz)
    if topk <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=int), np.empty(0, dtype=int)

    if topk == sz:
        order = np.argsort(-contrib)
    else:
        part = np.argpartition(contrib, -topk)[-topk:]
        order = part[np.argsort(-contrib[part])]

    return src[order], tgt[order]


def choose_pivot(h, rng):
    score = np.minimum(h, 1.0 - h)
    mx = float(score.max())
    cand = np.where(score >= max(0.0, mx - 1e-12))[0]
    if cand.size == 0:
        return 0
    return int(rng.choice(cand))


def get_pair_params(n):
    if n <= 650:
        return 31, 5, 11, 60
    elif n <= 1300:
        return 21, 4, 9, 40
    else:
        return 15, 3, 7, 25


def run_targeted_pair_moves(h0, n, deadline, seed,
                            grid_num, refine_levels, refine_num, max_pairs):
    h = project_feasible(h0, n).copy()
    c = compute_raw_c(h)
    best_c = compute_c5_raw(c, n)
    best_h = h.copy()

    work = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    gi = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    gj = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    lin = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)

    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    stale = 0
    outer = 0

    while time.time() < deadline:
        outer += 1
        cmat = c
        cmax = float(np.max(cmat))
        if cmax <= 0.0:
            break

        active = np.argsort(-cmat)[:min(10, len(cmat))]
        active = np.asarray(active, dtype=int)
        N = n - 1
        if N not in active and cmat[N] >= 0.95 * cmax:
            active = np.append(active, N)

        raw_pairs = []
        for m in active:
            src, tgt = get_shift_pairs(h, n, int(m), 8)
            for ii in range(src.size):
                i = int(src[ii])
                j = int(tgt[ii])
                if i == j:
                    continue
                if i < j:
                    raw_pairs.append((i, j))
                else:
                    raw_pairs.append((j, i))

        for _ in range(8):
            i = int(rng.randint(n))
            j = int(rng.randint(n))
            if i != j:
                if i < j:
                    raw_pairs.append((i, j))
                else:
                    raw_pairs.append((j, i))

        seen = set()
        pairs = []
        for p in raw_pairs:
            if p not in seen:
                seen.add(p)
                pairs.append(p)

        if len(pairs) > max_pairs:
            diff_scores = np.empty(len(pairs), dtype=float)
            for idx, (i, j) in enumerate(pairs):
                diff_scores[idx] = abs(float(h[i]) - float(h[j]))
            idxs = np.argpartition(diff_scores, max_pairs - 1)[:max_pairs]
            sel_pairs = []
            for idx in idxs:
                sel_pairs.append(pairs[int(idx)])
            pairs = sel_pairs

        improved = False
        moves = 0

        for (i, j) in pairs:
            if time.time() >= deadline:
                break
            if abs(float(h[i]) - float(h[j])) < 1e-12:
                continue
            if do_pair_move(h, c, i, j, n,
                            grid_num, refine_levels, refine_num,
                            work, gi, gj, lin):
                improved = True
                moves += 1
                if moves % 20 == 0:
                    c = compute_raw_c(h)

        c = compute_raw_c(h)
        cur = compute_c5_raw(c, n)
        if cur < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_c = cur
            best_h = h.copy()
            stale = 0
        else:
            stale += 1
            if stale >= 5:
                break

    cur = compute_c5(h, n)
    if cur < best_c:
        best_c = cur
        best_h = h.copy()

    return best_h, best_c


def run_pivot_sweeps(h0, n, deadline, seed,
                     grid_num, refine_levels, refine_num, max_pairs):
    h = project_feasible(h0, n).copy()
    c = compute_raw_c(h)
    best_c = compute_c5_raw(c, n)
    best_h = h.copy()

    work = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    gi = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    gj = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    lin = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)

    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    while time.time() < deadline:
        p = choose_pivot(h, rng)
        order = rng.permutation(n)
        improved = False
        moves = 0

        for i in order:
            if time.time() >= deadline:
                break
            i = int(i)
            if i == p:
                continue
            if abs(float(h[i]) - float(h[p])) < 1e-12:
                continue
            if do_pair_move(h, c, i, p, n,
                            grid_num, refine_levels, refine_num,
                            work, gi, gj, lin):
                improved = True
                moves += 1
                if moves % 50 == 0:
                    c = compute_raw_c(h)

        c = compute_raw_c(h)
        cur = compute_c5_raw(c, n)
        if cur < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_c = cur
            best_h = h.copy()

        if not improved:
            break

    cur = compute_c5(h, n)
    if cur < best_c:
        best_c = cur
        best_h = h.copy()

    return best_h, best_c


def smooth_perturb(h, n, eps, rng):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    if n <= 1:
        return project_feasible(h, n)
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    d = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    maxf = min(6, max(1, n // 3))
    for f in range(1, maxf + 1):
        amp = eps / float(f)
        p1 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        p2 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        w = (2.0 * np.pi * float(f) * idx) / float(n)
        d += amp * (np.sin(w + p1) + np.cos(w + p2))
    d -= float(d.mean())
    mx = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
    if mx > 1e-15:
        scale = min(1.0, (3.0 * eps) / mx)
        if scale < 1.0:
            d *= scale
    return project_feasible(h + d, n)


def find_active_indices(c, top_k, rel_tol):
    rawmax = float(c.max())
    threshold = rawmax - max(1e-8, rel_tol * rawmax)
    act = np.where(c >= threshold)[0]
    if act.size == 0:
        act = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)
    if act.size > top_k:
        sub = c[act]
        idx = np.argsort(-sub)
        act = act[idx[:top_k]]
    return np.unique(act.astype(int))


def slqp_obj(z, n, active, dx):
    return float(z[-1])


def slqp_obj_grad(z, n, active, dx):
    g = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=float)
    g[-1] = 1.0
    return g


def slqp_eq(z, n):
    return float(z[:-1].sum() - 0.5 * float(n))


def slqp_eq_grad(z, n):
    g = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=float)
    g[0:n] = 1.0
    return g


def slqp_ineq(z, n, active, dx):
    raw = compute_raw_c(z[:-1])
    return z[-1] - raw[active] * dx


def slqp_ineq_grad(z, n, active, dx):
    h = z[:-1]
    nn = int(len(h))
    N = nn - 1
    J = np.zeros((len(active), nn + 1), dtype=float)

    for row, m in enumerate(active):
        k = int(m) - N
        r = np.zeros(nn, dtype=float)
        if k >= 0:
            nv = nn - k
            if nv > 0:
                r[0:nv] += -h[k:nn]
                r[k:nn] += 1.0 - h[0:nv]
        else:
            kk = -k
            nv = nn - kk
            if nv > 0:
                r[kk:nn] += -h[0:nv]
                r[0:nv] += 1.0 - h[kk:nn]
        J[row, 0:nn] = -dx * r

    J[:, -1] = 1.0
    return J


def run_slqp_active(h0, n, deadline, seed, max_rounds=4):
    h = project_feasible(h0, n).copy()
    c = compute_raw_c(h)
    best_c = compute_c5_raw(c, n)
    best_h = h.copy()

    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    rounds = 0
    while time.time() < deadline - 5 and rounds < max_rounds:
        remaining = deadline - time.time()
        if remaining < 12.0:
            break

        c = compute_raw_c(best_h)
        N = n - 1
        rawmax = float(c.max())
        active = find_active_indices(c, top_k=12, rel_tol=0.02)
        if (N not in active) and c[N] >= 0.90 * rawmax:
            active = np.unique(np.append(active, N))

        if active.size == 0:
            active = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)

        C0 = rawmax * dx
        t0 = C0 + 1e-7
        if t0 > 0.999:
            t0 = 0.999

        y0 = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=float)
        y0[0:n] = best_h
        y0[n] = t0

        bounds = []
        for _ in range(n):
            bounds.append((0.0, 1.0))
        bounds.append((0.0, 1.0))

        cons = [
            {"type": "eq", "fun": slqp_eq, "jac": slqp_eq_grad, "args": (n,)},
            {"type": "ineq", "fun": slqp_ineq, "jac": slqp_ineq_grad,
             "args": (n, active, dx)},
        ]

        if n <= 700:
            base = 120
        elif n <= 1300:
            base = 70
        else:
            base = 45

        maxit = max(25, min(base, int(remaining * 0.45)))

        try:
            res = minimize(
                slqp_obj,
                y0,
                jac=slqp_obj_grad,
                args=(n, active, dx),
                method="SLSQP",
                bounds=bounds,
                constraints=cons,
                options={"maxiter": maxit, "ftol": 1e-13}
            )
            h_sol = project_feasible(res.x[:-1], n)
            c_sol = compute_raw_c(h_sol)
            c5_sol = compute_c5_raw(c_sol, n)

            if c5_sol < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_c = c5_sol
                best_h = h_sol.copy()
                rounds = 0
            else:
                rounds += 1
        except Exception:
            break

    cur = compute_c5(best_h, n)
    if cur < best_c:
        best_c = cur
        best_h = best_h.copy()

    return best_h, best_c


def ensure_work_size(h, n, max_n):
    if n <= max_n:
        return project_feasible(h, n).copy(), n
    hh = resample_h(h, max_n)
    return hh, max_n


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    deadline = t0 + max(1.0, budget_s - 6.0)

    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    parent = None
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None and parent.size > 0:
        h_orig = project_feasible(parent, len(parent)).copy()
    else:
        h_orig = project_feasible(make_fallback_start(), 512).copy()

    n_orig = int(len(h_orig))
    c_orig = compute_c5(h_orig, n_orig)

    best_h = h_orig.copy()
    best_c = c_orig
    best_n = n_orig

    OPT_MAX = 1200

    def phase_candidate_from_best():
        nonlocal best_h, best_c, best_n
        ph, pn = ensure_work_size(best_h, best_n, OPT_MAX)
        cp = compute_c5(ph, pn)
        if cp > best_c + 0.02:
            return None, None
        return ph, pn

    if n_orig > OPT_MAX:
        ph0, pn0 = ensure_work_size(h_orig, n_orig, OPT_MAX)
        c0 = compute_c5(ph0, pn0)
        if c0 < best_c:
            best_h = ph0.copy()
            best_c = c0
            best_n = pn0
    else:
        ph0 = best_h.copy()
        pn0 = best_n
        c0 = best_c

    grid0 = get_pair_params(pn0)

    if time.time() < deadline - 300:
        td = min(time.time() + 280.0, deadline - 260.0)
        h1, c1 = run_targeted_pair_moves(ph0, pn0, td, seed + 1, *grid0)
        if c1 < best_c - 1e-14:
            best_h = h1.copy()
            best_c = c1
            best_n = pn0

    if time.time() < deadline - 200:
        ph, pn = phase_candidate_from_best()
        if ph is not None:
            grid = get_pair_params(pn)
            td = min(time.time() + 130.0, deadline - 160.0)
            h2, c2 = run_pivot_sweeps(ph, pn, td, seed + 11, *grid)
            if c2 < best_c - 1e-14:
                best_h = h2.copy()
                best_c = c2
                best_n = pn

    if time.time() < deadline - 140:
        ph, pn = phase_candidate_from_best()
        if ph is not None:
            h3, c3 = run_slqp_active(ph, pn, min(time.time() + 90.0, deadline - 120.0), seed + 21, max_rounds=3)
            if c3 < best_c - 1e-14:
                best_h = h3.copy()
                best_c = c3
                best_n = pn

    if time.time() < deadline - 180:
        hb = binary_round_start(best_h, best_n)
        cb = compute_c5(hb, best_n)
        if cb < best_c + 0.004:
            if cb < best_c:
                best_h = hb.copy()
                best_c = cb
            grid = get_pair_params(best_n)
            td = min(time.time() + 70.0, deadline - 140.0)
            h4, c4 = run_targeted_pair_moves(hb, best_n, td, seed + 31, *grid)
            if c4 < best_c - 1e-14:
                best_h = h4.copy()
                best_c = c4
                best_n = best_n

    restarts = 0
    while time.time() < deadline - 230 and restarts < 3:
        eps = 0.035 + 0.025 * restarts
        ph, pn = phase_candidate_from_best()
        if ph is None:
            break
        hp = smooth_perturb(ph, pn, eps, rng)
        cp = compute_c5(hp, pn)
        if cp > best_c + 0.012:
            restarts += 1
            continue

        grid = get_pair_params(pn)
        td = min(time.time() + 110.0, deadline - 160.0)
        h5, c5 = run_targeted_pair_moves(hp, pn, td, seed + 100 + restarts, *grid)
        if c5 < best_c - 1e-14:
            best_h = h5.copy()
            best_c = c5
            best_n = pn

        if time.time() < deadline - 90:
            h6, c6 = run_slqp_active(hp, pn, min(time.time() + 50.0, deadline - 80.0),
                                     seed + 200 + restarts, max_rounds=2)
            if c6 < best_c - 1e-14:
                best_h = h6.copy()
                best_c = c6
                best_n = pn

        restarts += 1

    if time.time() < deadline - 170 and best_n < 1000:
        n_fine = 1000
        hf = resample_h(best_h, n_fine)
        cf = compute_c5(hf, n_fine)
        if cf < best_c + 0.008:
            grid_f = get_pair_params(n_fine)
            td = min(time.time() + 150.0, deadline - 90.0)
            hf2, cf2 = run_targeted_pair_moves(hf, n_fine, td, seed + 300, *grid_f)
            if cf2 < best_c - 1e-14:
                best_h = hf2.copy()
                best_c = cf2
                best_n = n_fine

            if time.time() < deadline - 70:
                hf3, cf3 = run_slqp_active(hf2, n_fine,
                                           min(time.time() + 55.0, deadline - 60.0),
                                           seed + 400, max_rounds=2)
                if cf3 < best_c - 1e-14:
                    best_h = hf3.copy()
                    best_c = cf3
                    best_n = n_fine

    if time.time() < deadline - 40:
        ph, pn = phase_candidate_from_best()
        if ph is not None:
            grid = get_pair_params(pn)
            td = min(time.time() + 25.0, deadline - 15.0)
            h7, c7 = run_targeted_pair_moves(ph, pn, td, seed + 500, *grid)
            if c7 < best_c - 1e-14:
                best_h = h7.copy()
                best_c = c7
                best_n = pn

    if time.time() < deadline - 12:
        ph, pn = phase_candidate_from_best()
        if ph is not None:
            h8, c8 = run_slqp_active(ph, pn, min(time.time() + 8.0, deadline - 3.0),
                                     seed + 600, max_rounds=1)
            if c8 < best_c - 1e-14:
                best_h = h8.copy()
                best_c = c8
                best_n = pn

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, best_n)
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, best_n)
    best_n = int(len(best_h))

    return best_h, best_c, best_n
```
